# Testing

Which tests run for which change, and where they live. The layers come from the design
(`docs/ko/desk-structure.md`, "QA: 테스트 층"). Layers are switched on when they have something real to
catch; until then they are marked "not yet".

## Layers

Image opening regressions: `just test-e2e image-files.spec.ts` loads the real Images and Explorer
plugins in the shell under the plugin CSP. It checks JPEG/PNG rendering, single-click opening while
Images is off, reopening after close, and opening the same or another image from the drawer. The
runtime's `plugin-reconnect.test.ts` checks requests wait for the recreated frame; Images'
`files.test.ts` checks surviving buffers reload without reopening buffers already closed.

| Layer | What it checks | Tool and location | When | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Static | fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, biome, tsc strict, actionlint, spec lint (Ajv strict compile of every schema, spectral for the OpenAPI), theme conformance (`scripts/ui-lint.mjs` ratchet), design system conformance (`scripts/ds-lint.mjs` ratchet) | `just check` | every PR (`ci`) | active |
| Codegen drift | `just codegen` reproduces the committed generated files | job `codegen-check` | every PR | active |
| Unit | functions and modules; the e2e runner's plan, run lease and Playwright guard, the session doctor and the agent hook (ADR 0018, ADR 0144); the app e2e's pinned chromedriver and the runtime e2e's pinned headless shell (`e2e/support/chrome-for-testing.test.ts`); the runtime's routing of turns, watches, data subscriptions and processes to their plugin (`owners.test.ts`); the stub core's file tree answering fs.stat, fs.mkdir, fs.rename, fs.copy and fs.trash as the core does, both ends' scopes, no overwrite, and fs.changed for the watches a move reaches (`packages/runtime/src/bridge/fs.test.ts`, ADR 0125); autoload plugins on the stub core (ADR 0046): `desk.files` turning one on for a file and off after its last buffer but never one the person turned on, across a restart too, and profiles leaving them as they are (`policies/files-autoload.test.ts`, `policies/profiles-autoload.test.ts`); the terminal plugin on the SDK's test host (sessions, split panes with their layout model, divider drags and the layout kept for a restart in `layout.test.ts`, `panes.test.ts` and `panes-view.test.ts`, shells, keys, theme with xterm's proposed API allowed, manifest) and the vendored emulator matching its pinned packages (`vendor-xterm.test.mjs`, ADR 0037); the Screen Sharing plugin (`plugins/screens/tests`, ADR 0133): its socket as noVNC's channel (what comes before the answer and before anyone listens kept in order, one `net.send` in flight with what queued meanwhile, clean and failed ends, noVNC's own Websock attached; `channel.test.ts`), the vendored noVNC's RFB over that channel signing in to a fake RFB 3.8 server with the VNC password and refused in words (`rfb.test.ts`), the session's one grant ask, sign-in, key trust, toggles and words for a silent or refusing host (`session.test.ts`), the kept computers without passwords (`computers.test.ts`), the page on jsdom (`view.test.ts`), its manifest, and the vendored copy matching its pin (`vendor-novnc.test.mjs`); the core's sockets against in-process TCP servers (`crates/desk-core/tests/sockets.rs`: the scope and the grant checked before anything connects, a port named or any port, bytes both ways in order over several `net.data`, the owner alone hearing them, `net.closed` with its reason, at most 8 per caller, closed by a revoke or a disabled plugin) and `net:connect`'s endpoint matching (`paths.rs`), as the browser stub judges it too (`stub-covers.test.ts`); the database plugin on the SDK's test host with a scripted psql and sqlite3 answering in their own protocols (connections and URIs, consoles, the read-only and production guards, passwords asked once and never kept, agents' commands), and its lexer, statement classes and engines' output readers on captured client output (ADR 0038), pending changes built as one transaction keyed on the primary key per dialect, refused without a key or on a read-only connection and rolled back on a failure (`pending-changes.test.ts`, ADR 0115), autocomplete from an introspected schema with aliases and highlighting (`autocomplete.test.ts`), statements at the caret with dollar quotes, comments, DELIMITER and MySQL routine bodies (`statement-at-cursor.test.ts`), Format SQL changing only space and the case of keywords and parameters filled as literals (`editor-tools.test.ts`), the DDL of every object kind from catalog fixtures (`ddl.test.ts`), copy and export formats and the export into ~/Downloads (`export.test.ts`), EXPLAIN output of PostgreSQL, MySQL, MariaDB and SQLite read as plan trees (`explain.test.ts`), the grid's order, the value viewer and drops (`grid-model.test.ts`), starred queries and the history searched (`saved-queries.test.ts`), parameters, Explain as a plan tab, Format, a console SELECT paged and a DDL view through the commands (`console-tools.test.ts`), connection groups and colours (`connection-groups.test.ts`), and the ER diagram's model and layout, and a real SQLite schema drawn (`er-diagram.test.ts`); the Docker plugin on the SDK's test host with a scripted docker (`plugins/docker/tests`: parsers on docker 29 output, names and OCI references, the runner and its PATH fallback, logs, the event stream and alerts, actions, the controller, the list renderers, keys, manifest; ADR 0039), and its redesign (ADR 0114): the overview model counting containers by state, grouping Compose stacks and adding up disk use (`overview.test.ts`), bulk actions with one docker call per marked container and partial failures (`bulk.test.ts`), search, filter and sort (`filter-sort.test.ts`), the `docker stats` stream's ANSI frames feeding the sparklines (`stats.test.ts`), the plugin's colours at 4.5:1 and 3:1 in light and dark (`contrast.test.ts`, NF-05), image history, pull and build progress and prune previews (`images.test.ts`), a container's files and `docker cp` (`files.test.ts`), reloads (`reload.test.ts`), and the surface's DOM wired from `index.html` on a fake document without jsdom (`view.test.ts`, `view-lists.test.ts`); the Local agents plugin on the SDK's test host with scripted agent programs behind `proc.spawn` (`plugins/local-agents/tests`: each program's arguments with its read-only flag and never a widening one, a prompt that starts with a dash kept an argument, the readers on Claude Code's, Cursor's and Gemini's stream-json, Codex's new and old JSON lines and Grok's messages, the runner with its PATH fallback, one shared permission ask, timeouts and stops, detection of installed, missing and broken programs, a resume that fails asked again with the turns in the prompt and a version without the structured flags asked again for plain text, the chat with sessions and the store's 64 KiB, the ask before running a program nothing holds read-only, compare, the board's columns, queue and parallel limit, the commands, and the five views on jsdom with drag and drop; ADR 0084); the nine file plugins on the SDK's test host (`plugins/{sheets,docs,slides,reader,images,player,data,archives,web}/tests`, views on jsdom; ADR 0045): each format's reader or decoder against fixtures written by real tools and made by each plugin's own script, Word 97-2003, HWP 5.0 and PowerPoint 97-2003 with their formatting (`plugins/docs/tests/{doc-binary,doc-format,hwp-binary,hwp-format}.test.ts`, `plugins/slides/tests/{ppt,ppt-format}.test.ts`; the `.doc` and `.ppt` against the `.docx` and `.pptx` of the same document), the details view for damaged, encrypted and unsupported files, the desk's file requests and the one folder ask, reads in ranges through the SDK's `readBytes` or `byteSource` with `<desk-progress>` after 300 ms and its Cancel (`progress.test.ts` in docs, slides, web, images, sheets, data, reader and archives), large files parsed or decoded in a worker started with `startWorker`, reading only the ranges they need (`worker.test.ts` and `large.test.ts` in sheets, data and reader, the worker cases of the docs, slides, web and images `progress.test.ts`; The desk at rest (#129, #134): three still surfaces long idle, which take no begin frames (ADR 0164), the probe's loop run as the window's runs, ten seconds of it, its wake-ups a second within 15 and the browser process's CPU within 4 % of a core, with its OS wake-ups, CEF's work, the begin frames, the GPU process's and the renderers' CPU kept for the trend; then a fourth page that changes itself by a timer, three times, each once it is long idle: the change to its paint within 100 ms at the median (its page asks for frames, ADR 0164). Presses on a desk at rest (#133): two click surfaces in turn, each press after the desk rested 2 to 3 s, so each pressed surface is long idle and nothing has painted for seconds; one press not counted, then three blocks of twelve: the press to its paint within 27 ms at p95 in the block whose p95 is the median (16 ms until ADR 0210 turned the full pipeline off; the GPU's wake-up decides it, not a begin frame, ADR 0231), as the machine's own cores move a block by several milliseconds (#148, ADR 0162). A baseline recorded on another platform or with another tool is reported, never failed | `just perf-compositor` (`record` sets the baselines; `just build-shell` once first), results in `e2e/test-results/perf-compositor` | by hand on the reference machine before a change to the compositor, the web host or the CEF pin lands; never on PRs; nightly once #61 has a runner with a GPU | active (memory per live surface in its band since #111; a press to its paint, p95, 4.9 to 9.2 ms in five runs of five since #117 on Chromium's full pipeline, ADR 0157; the desk at rest woke 34.6 to 36.7 times a second in five probe runs since #129, against 54.6 to 57.7 with main's pump, ADR 0159; a press on a desk at rest 12.9 to 16.1 ms at p95 in nine runs since #133, against 10.2 to 23.3 on main, ADR 0162; without the full pipeline since #248, ADR 0210, on a quiet machine, a press's desk frames held to its first prompt, on `main` 858309f2: a press to its paint 15.6 to 20.4 ms at p95 in eleven runs, its composed frame 15.7 to 27.2 at the median (about 13 to 17 or 24 to 27 a run, #268), a press on a desk at rest 13.1 to 24.3; the full pipeline between them 6.6 to 8.0, 13.4 to 15.4 and 13.6 to 16.3; since ADR 0231, on `main` b30d0628 at a load average of 6.2 to 14.4, a press to its paint 3.8 to 8.7 ms at p95 in twelve runs and its composed frame 12.5 to 16.2 at the median, where `main` beside them read 15.7 to 23.0 and 14.1 to 27.6) |
| Performance gate: the compositor in motion, the test for every window change | ADR 0205, ADR 0208 (#247) and ADR 0213 (#258): the optimized shell with its development commands on (`--release` with debug assertions), a fresh desk on the Default profile's 13 plugin windows at 1280×800, headless (`DESK_COMPOSITOR_HEADLESS=1`: each frame rendered off screen, so no screen, pointer or keyboard of the machine's is used), the Plugins window shaken by `compositor_input { shake }` (2 Hz, ±200 px, a move every 8 ms, 4 s), gripped between its title's icon and tools and first dragged to the desk's middle when the profile's layout leaves it no room to swing there clear of the bars at the desk's sides (#331) while `DESK_FRAME_TRACE` writes each frame, each scene's windows and each frame read back, and a third time with the main thread held 20 ms right after a frame every 250 ms (`compositor_input { busy }`, #249); three runs, the worst judged against `e2e/perf/motion.json`: at least the frames a second its display's rate allows while the window moves (58 at 60 Hz, 110 at 120, the display the trace names, ADR 0235); intervals over 25 ms at most 1 %; no frame dropped across a busy loop (`busy`: an interval of a display interval and a half); no mid-motion frame (ten read back a run) where the window shows pixels not its own (blocks of 8 device pixels whose mean moved by more than 24 of 255, 4 blocks or more); every page in sight painting within 1.5 s of a DevTools poke, none found stalled or replaced, the desk not restarted; a window dropped at (+0.4, +0.6) at least 0.95 as sharp as at a whole pixel (a known failure until #250 put windows at rest on whole device pixels). Then, each run, the scenario a person judges a window change by (ADR 0213): the app window's frame on two sines, ±300 points wide over 700 ms and ±200 high over 1 s, a new frame every 8 ms for 8 s, set from the event loop by `compositor_window { sway }` as a person's live resize reaches the shell (the shell does not treat AppKit's live resize apart: both come as `Resized` to `CompositorWindow::resized`), from 1024×600 to 1624×1000, the Terminal snapped to the right half (its picture changes size with the app) and the Plugins window shaken from before the sway to after it. Its checks, `sway-` in the gate's table: frames a second and long intervals as above; no mid-motion frame with pixels not the shaken window's, leaving out where the system layer lies over it; no frame where a window's picture, or the desk page's against its scene, is laid out for another size than it is drawn at; no frame where a window's own page lays its section out for another size than the page has, as each page notes it through DevTools (`sway-sections`, ADR 0214); no page that stops painting, the desk's own included, and no ADR 0202 restart, in the trace or the log; once the app window rests, every window at its place, inside the app, drawn from its own page with a picture of its size; the shaken window as sharp after the sway as at a whole point. Last, each run, the resize loop (ADR 0210, #248: `e2e/perf/motion-loop.ts`): the app window resized by four sizes at once and a plugin window maximized or restored, each in turn, a turn every 100 ms for 100 turns, then a poke; check `loop`: no page that stops painting in or after it, the desk's own included, and no ADR 0202 restart (the desk's catch-ups are counted, not failed); the desk captured at its middle turn and after it (`loop-mid.png`, `loop-rest.png`). After each run the gate ends every process that runs from its bundle, whatever its parent, a desk started again for a stall (ADR 0202) included, which can start after its parent quit and outlive it under launchd; it ends what an earlier, stopped run of the bundle left before it starts, and fails if any process of the bundle is left at its end. A check that fails on `main` is listed with the issue that fixes it: under `known` when it fails every time, under `sometimes` (reported, never failing the gate) when it comes and goes. Its judges' unit tests (`e2e/perf/lib/motion.test.ts`, `e2e/perf/lib/motion-sway.test.ts`, `e2e/perf/lib/motion-sections.test.ts`, `e2e/perf/lib/bundle-processes.test.ts`) in `just test-unit`, the trace's and the sway's own (`apps/desk/shell/src/frame_trace.rs`, `apps/desk/shell/src/compositor_window/motion.rs`) in `cargo test -p desk-shell` | `just perf-motion` (`--no-build` with the shell built, `--debug` the development build for the record, never failing, `--screen` presented to the window on an unlocked screen, `record` sets the baseline), results and every run's trace, log and frames in `e2e/test-results/perf-motion/<variant>` | by hand on the reference machine before every change to how windows are placed, drawn, resized or painted lands: the compositor, `desk-wm`, `compositor_window`, the web host, the shell's windows (Which change runs what); never on PRs until #61 has a runner with a GPU | active since #247, the sway since #258, the loop since #248 (the baselines below; three runs take 219 s once built) |

The reference machine for budgets is the maintainers' Mac, an Apple M4 Max on macOS 26 (`darwin-arm64`),
the one `just perf-opening`, `just perf-compositor` and #35 recorded on. Record on it when it is quiet:
#58's baselines were taken at a load average of 3 to 6; at 20 to 38 (other builds on the same Mac) the
starts measured up to a third slower, past their allowance. To see where a frame's time goes,
`DESK_PROBE_TRACE=<file>` makes any surface probe run write Chromium's trace of its scenario (JSON for
`chrome://tracing` or Perfetto; `DESK_PROBE_TRACE_CATEGORIES` for others than the frame pipeline's),
e.g. after `just build-shell`, `DESK_PROBE_TRACE=/tmp/input.json HOME=$(mktemp -d)
target/bundle/endue-desk.app/Contents/MacOS/endue-desk --surface-probe=input` (`target/debug/endue-desk` off
macOS) (#117).

On 2026-11-21 the render thread (#249, ADR 0235, 97024585) measured, in five runs at a load of 3 to 7: 59.6 frames a second, no frame dropped across 80 busy loops, 0 % intervals over 25 ms; the sway 59.5 frames a second and 0.8 % (p95 21.4 ms), where `main` d8432e01 had 2.1 % and 10.9 % at load 3. `sway-smooth` is out of `known` since.

The motion gate's baseline (`e2e/perf/motion.json`, `just perf-motion record`) was taken on `main` 9dfca4dc
with #247's trace and shake, on the reference machine on 2026-11-14, at a load average of 14 to 36 (other
agents' builds on the same Mac; record again when it is quiet), the worst of three runs each:

| variant | frames a second | intervals over 25 ms | interval p95 / max | frames with pixels not the window's | pages that stopped | sharpness at rest |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| optimized, headless (the gate) | 59.8 | 0.4 % | 20.3 / 27.5 ms | 0 of 30 | none | 0.774 (known, #250) |
| development build, headless (`--debug`, for the record) | 59.7 | 0.4 % | 20.2 / 26.6 ms | 0 of 30 | the Terminal's page once, 3.5 s after the start, before any shake (the #157 watch loaded it again) | 0.774 |
| optimized, presented to the window (`--screen`) | 59.9 | 0.8 % | 20.0 / 29.4 ms | 0 of 30 | none | 0.774 |

The same shake on `main` 4e140552 a day earlier (before ADR 0201 gave each window its own page) found 39.7
frames a second in the development build and 58.0 optimized, one frame in four with another window's pixels
and the desk's page stalled in two runs of five. The gate shakes only once the desk's page has not painted
for 1.5 s: shaken during its start (it paints 60 times a second for its first seconds), the same build
measured 43 to 54 frames a second at that load.

The gate as it runs since #258, the single shake and then the sway, was recorded again on `main` 6e75d95e
on the reference machine on 2026-11-14 at a load average of 3.7 to 4.3, optimized and headless, the worst
of three runs (ADR 0213 explains each value). The single shake: 59.6 frames a second, 1.7 % of intervals
over 25 ms (p95 20.6 ms, max 32.3 ms), no frame of 30 with pixels not the window's, no page stopped, 1.0
sharp at rest (#250's soft drop is gone on this `main`). The sway, the app window from 1024×600 to
1624×1000:

| check | value | verdict on `main` 6e75d95e |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `sway-fps`: frames a second while both move | 58.4 | pass |
| `sway-smooth`: intervals over 25 ms | 10.7 % (p95 28 ms, max 49.9 ms) | known, #249 |
| `sway-own-pixels`: frames with pixels not the shaken window's | 2 of 30 (the desk's bars drawn where its scene no longer has them) | at times, #252 |
| `sway-sizes`: frames where a window's picture is laid out for another size | 1148 of 1408 (the snapped Terminal's, cut or padded) | known, #252 |
| `sway-scene`: frames where the desk's page is laid out for another size than its scene | 670 of 1408 | known, #252 |
| `sway-painting`: pages that stopped painting | the desk's own in run 3, then an ADR 0202 restart | at times, #248 |
| `sway-settled`: windows off their place, the app or their picture's size once it rests | none | pass |
| `sway-soft`: sharpness at rest after the sway | 0.99 | pass |

The desk's page stopped in 3 of 10 sways that day, the line the person's desk logged on 2026-11-13 after
a live resize: the sway reproduces what single resizes had not. A fix takes its check out of `known` or
`sometimes`; a check under `sometimes` is fixed only once several gate runs pass it. The development build
and the presented variant keep #247's baselines above and have not run the sway (the presented one would
resize a window on the person's screen).

Since #248 (ADR 0210: Chromium's full pipeline off, the desk catching a page up) the gate ends each run
with the resize loop, and `sway-painting` is no longer under `sometimes`. On `main` 4d76dcaa with that
change, five runs at a load average of 6 to 40, the worst of five: the single shake 59.9 frames a second,
0.8 % of intervals over 25 ms (p95 20.8 ms, max 39.9 ms), no frame of 50 with pixels not the window's, no
page stopped, 1.0 sharp; the sway 55.2 frames a second, 13.8 % over 25 ms (p95 30.9 ms, max 65 ms; known,
#249), 1 frame of 50 with pixels not the shaken window's (at times, #252), 1995 and 550 of 2243 frames
laid out for another size (known, #252), no page stopped and no restart, every window settled, 0.99
sharp; the loop's 500 turns: no page stopped, none loaded again, no restart (94 to 125 catch-ups a run).
With the full pipeline the desk's page stopped in the loop in every run. On `main` 858309f2 (#263 in)
with a press's desk frames held to its first prompt, ten runs in two batches at a load average of 4.5 to
15.9: no page stopped in the shake or the sway and the desk never started again; the loop's 900 turns
stopped one terminal page once (the stall watch, at a load average of 14), and one run's desk was ended
from outside mid-sway (no restart, no crash report, nothing in its log); the sway 52.4 to 57.6 frames a
second, under 55 only at a load average of 14 and 16, and the single shake's intervals over 25 ms 2.5 and
3 % at 11 to 14. Since then the gate ends every process of its bundle after each run and fails if one is
left at its end: a run with the full pipeline, whose desk started again in the loop (ADR 0202), ended
that desk, its parent launchd by then, with the rest.

Since #270 (ADR 0214: a window's section fills its own page, and its page told its screen paints once)
the sway also has each window's own page note its size and its section's at every frame where either
changes (`sway-sections`). On the reference machine on 2026-11-14, three runs a set with this gate: the
shell and runtime of `main` 858309f2, at a load average of 3.9 to 4.7, laid a window's section out for
another size than its page in 462 of 1453 frames (129 to 193 a run, all the snapped Terminal's, its
section up to 106 px off its page); with this change, in none of the 1403 to 1433 frames of each of two
sets. The first, at a load average of 5.8 rising to 52 (another session's build), read as on `main`:
the shake 59.4 frames a second and 1.3 % over 25 ms, no page stopped in the loop, `sway-sizes` 0,
`sway-scene` and `sway-own-pixels` as #269 left them, `sway-smooth` as #249 left it, every window
settled, 0.99 sharp. The second, started at 11.5, went 2.1 % over 25 ms in the shake and lost the
Terminal's plugin page in the loop's first run (ADR 0210's stall, seen in 1 of 23 runs of other builds
that day).

Since #269 (ADR 0215: the desk's page drawn with the scene its picture was laid out for) `sway-scene`
left `known` and `sway-own-pixels` left `sometimes`, and `sway-own-pixels` also leaves out the reach of
a floating part's shadow past its box (`SHADOW` in `e2e/perf/lib/motion.ts`, the talk pill's
`--desk-shadow-md`). On `main` 858309f2 with that change, two sets of five runs at a load average of 2
to 5: `sway-scene` 0 of 2309 and 0 of 2304 frames (337 of 1352 on `main`), `sway-own-pixels` 0 of 50,
the sway 56.9 frames a second, `sway-smooth` 13.6 and 17.1 % (known, #249); the loop failed in one run of
ten, a plugin page held at one size until the stall watch loaded it again (ADR 0210's fault).

#345 (ADR 0279, proposed) looked for a step before that reload. On `main` a6a50ba9 the loop alone
(`runLoop` and `analyzeLoop`, the Terminal snapped right first, three desks of ten loops an arm on the
reference Mac at a load of 15 to 80) stopped a page in 13 of 120 loops, any page, not the Terminal's
alone. A stuck page's view gets no begin frame from CEF again; one stuck at its size comes back when
its browser hears its screen (the stall watch's `asked` step), one stuck off its size comes back only
when loaded again, and fifteen other calls to its browser did not bring it back. A hide that reaches
CEF only a second after the surface is hidden (2 of 30), a view with a size in flight kept shown to
CEF (3 of 30), every window kept live (2 of 30) and the catch-up off (2 of 30) did not lower the
rate. ADR 0279 proposes listing `loop` under `sometimes` with #345; `e2e/perf/motion.json` is as it was.

## Usability checks

These checks cover the usability changes merged in November 2026. Linked PRs retain the detailed
results and screenshots; fixture screenshots are identified separately from native-app observations.

| Area | Automated checks | Native or manual evidence and limits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Native file entry | `apps/desk/src/native-files.test.ts` and `packages/runtime/src/policies/files.test.ts`: picker cancellation, absolute paths, queued and deduplicated drops, failed-file isolation, disposal, and `desk.files.open` routing | [PR #219](https://github.com/endue-ai/Desk/pull/219): app startup, permissions and buffer reuse checked. Physical OS-dialog interaction and pointer drops were unavailable on the locked Mac; adapter tests and three-platform app builds cover the implemented paths. |
| Drag and drop between plugins | `packages/runtime/src/dnd/broker.test.ts`, `packages/plugin-sdk/src/drag.test.ts`, `plugins/{explorer,notes}/tests/dnd.test.ts`, `apps/desk/src/native-files.test.ts` and `packages/runtime/e2e/dnd.spec.ts` (ADR 0119) | [Issue #544](https://github.com/endue-ai/Desk/issues/544): the runtime e2e drags in Chromium across real frames; a Finder drop and WebView2 are not observed on this branch (the app was not launched: shared Mac). |
| OS file associations | `apps/desk/src/{open-files,file-associations}.test.ts` and `apps/desk/shell/src/open_files.rs`: requests wait for runtime readiness, batch isolation, command-line paths, and the CEF desk's declared viewers (`apps/desk/shell/packager/macos/Info.plist`, `linux/desk.desktop`). A YAML showed "YAML · Text editing"; its first save asked Save as copy (selected) or Overwrite, and Save as copy wrote `service.copy.yaml` with only that line changed, its comment kept, and left `service.yaml` as it was; revert read it again. |

Historical timing and browser failures remain in the PR evidence. [Issue #226](https://github.com/endue-ai/Desk/issues/226)
records the DjVu timer-gap failures; [PR #234](https://github.com/endue-ai/Desk/pull/234) separates yielded
waits from active render segments while preserving the 60 ms ceiling, the 25 ms pacer policy, real
timer turns and cancellation checks. Negative controls catch excessive work before and after a yield.
[Issue #227](https://github.com/endue-ai/Desk/issues/227) tracks local Docker WebKit crashes and onboarding
deadlines. [PR #235](https://github.com/endue-ai/Desk/pull/235) adds Player fixture teardown and regressions
that observe zero playback timers after every mounted view unloads, including detached views. No
production playback interval, timeout or required CI gate was relaxed.

[PR #237](https://github.com/endue-ai/Desk/pull/237) confines the accelerated lease clock to explicit
expiry checks. Ordinary HTTP conformance conversations use the server's normal clock; the same
lease TTLs, response schemas and expiry assertions remain. A regression keeps a 60-second lease
through a deliberate 1.5-second validation pause before completing a real HTTP turn.

[PR #250](https://github.com/endue-ai/Desk/pull/250) removes startup fixture polling after the awaited
Mail plugin mount. `plugins/mail/tests/keyboard.test.ts` holds initialization responses and checks
startup readiness without advancing timers. Existing keyboard, invitation and read-state assertions,
test timeouts and the general follow-up settle helper remain. This removes observed fixed startup
waiting; it does not establish the cause of the earlier Windows 5-second timeout.

## Main-thread work in plugins

The audit of #38 (ADR 0048): every plugin under `plugins/`, for synchronous work on its frame's main
thread that can pass 16 ms on a realistic input. In WebKit every frame shares the shell's main thread,
so such work stalls the whole desk. "Now" is before #38, "after" is with it. Times marked Node are V8
on a laptop (a lower bound for WebKit); e2e times are the runtime e2e's WebKit in its Linux container.
`scripts/plugin-workers.test.mjs` holds the table to the code: it fails when a plugin folder has no
row, when a parser or decoder listed in its `OFF_MAIN` (database, notes, player, sheets) is loaded on
the main thread from `ui/main.js`, or when a worker module imports a bare specifier (import maps do
not reach workers). `packages/runtime/e2e/perf/heavy-open.spec.ts` times the shell's thread while
Notes opens a 4 MiB note and types in it and while Explorer previews a 7.1 MiB image (`?heavy=1`).

| Plugin | Heavy path | Where it runs now | After | Fixture size tested |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `agents` | Chat transcript (≤ 200 turns × 8000 chars); every turn redrawn with its markdown on each streamed step | Frame (DOM) | Stays: bounded input; the redraw is DOM work, not a worker's (follow-up: redraw the streaming turn only) | Read, not run |
| `api-studio` | Response bodies (curl stops at 64 MiB), pretty JSON and its colours, the JSON tree, test scripts | Frame; scripts in a blob worker (ten seconds) | Stays: bodies past 2 MB draw plain, the tree draws 2,000 children a node until asked, find marks at most 5,000 matches | `engine.test.ts` (real curl), `viewer.dom.test.ts`, `scripts.test.ts`, `studio.dom.test.ts`, `security.test.ts`, `redirects.test.ts`, `literals.test.ts`, `trust.dom.test.ts` |
| `archives` | Listings and extraction (ZIP, 7z, RAR, disk images) through `endue.formats` | Library worker; the frame only when no worker starts | Unchanged (confirmed) | PR #261 fixtures |
| `browser` | None: the page is a web view of the desk's in a browser of its own (ADR 0216); the frame holds a toolbar and parses one address | — | — | — |
| `buffers` | None: a policy, no frame | — | — | — |
| `calendar` | `.ics` parse, the model (VTIMEZONE expansion), recurrence expansion on every render and each minute, save | Frame: 50 MB parse 295 ms, model 933 ms, one render's occurrences 1.2 s (Node) | Stays for now: a worker parse alone would not help, the model holds closures and every render expands it; follow-up: the model in a worker | 1, 10, 50 MB `.ics` (Node) |
| `chat` | Messages (≤ 500 a page), markup cached per message | Frame | Stays: a 2 KB message parses in 0.1 ms; an `Intl.DateTimeFormat` per message per render costs 32 ms at 500 (follow-up) | 500 messages (Node) |
| `chat-digest` | None: a data-only view plugin | — | — | — |
| `computer` | Accessibility tree ≤ 300 nodes; screenshots passed on as base64, never decoded | Frame | Stays: bounded | Read, not run |
| `data` | JSON, YAML, TOML, XML and nine more parsed; tree, raw, search | Worker over 1 MiB, frame below | Unchanged (confirmed; out of scope) | 50 MiB JSON (PR #229) |
| `database` | Client output (psql CSV, mysql XML, sqlite3 quote mode) decoded and parsed, up to 64 MiB; a dropped CSV or JSON parsed for an import (≤ 16 MiB); sort and filter over up to 100k kept rows | Frame | Output: `ui/output-worker.js`, the runner's bytes go there and sets come back cut to the row limit; bar after 300 ms with Cancel, which stops the worker. The import's parse and the grid's sort and filter stay (follow-up) | 50 MiB psql output: frame 0.1 ms, worker 0.9 s (Node) |

| `design-soft` | None: a design system, data only | — | — | — |
| `display` | None: a policy, no frame | — | — | — |

| `docker-studio` | Each list's inspect JSON (the runner stops docker at 16 MiB), stats and events lines, log lines per frame into a ring of 5,000, build steps | Frame | Stays: a list is one inspect of what ls named; log lines are batched per animation frame and only the new ones drawn; a search or level change redraws the ring (DOM, bounded by its size) | `parse.test.ts`, `logs-files.test.ts`, the preview on the seeded engine |
| `docs` | DOCX, ODT, RTF, HWP, DOC and more parsed | Worker over 8 MiB, frame below | Unchanged (confirmed; out of scope; follow-up: a lower threshold) | PR #253 fixtures |

| `explorer` | Image preview read as base64 (≤ 8 MiB) and decoded with `atob` in the frame (0.3 s in Node); every expanded folder sorted again on each render | Frame | The frame loads the image from its `fs.url` URL; the engine decodes it (`img.decode()` before it shows); text previews stay (≤ 64 KiB, < 2 ms). The sort stays: fine at hundreds of entries, an estimated 0.5–2 s a render at 100k, not measured (follow-up: sort once per listing) | 7.1 MiB PNG (e2e, warm: longest 57–139 ms now, 13–24 ms after) |

| `inspector` | Tables ≤ 2000 rows redrawn every 2–5 s; export JSON once | Frame | Stays: bounded | Golden export |
| `item` | None: a data-only view plugin | — | — | — |
| `launcher` | None: a list of at most a few dozen plugins, filtered and drawn as tiles | Frame | Stays: bounded input | — |
| `local-agents` | stream-json lines ≤ 256 KiB, `JSON.parse` per line; the answer's markdown redrawn every 40 ms | Frame | Stays: per line; the redraw is DOM (follow-up) | Read, not run |
| `local-models` | GGUF headers read in ranges (`fs.read` offset and length, never the whole file); streamed answers parsed per event-stream or NDJSON line and drawn once per animation frame; text attachments (at most 2 MB) cut into passages of about 1,000 characters and embedded 24 at a time | Frame | Stays: a header read touches only the header, answers redraw at most once a frame, and a long file is embedded once and its vectors kept in `~/.endue/local-models/rag/` | `engine.test.ts` (GGUF headers), `transport.test.ts`, `attach.test.ts`, `chat.dom.test.ts` |
| `login` | None: a policy, no frame; the shell registers the login item | — | — | — |
| `mail` | Invitation (iTIP) parse; list rows | Frame | Stays: an invitation parses in 0.13 ms; a render per changed item and an `Intl` format per row are not parsing (follow-up) | 1000-event invitation (Node) |
| `market` | None: a policy, no frame | — | — | — |
| `memory` | ≤ 200 entries | Frame | Stays: bounded | — |
| `network` | The OUI table (about 40,000 lines, 800 KB) parsed once into a map; a /24 sweep's 253 ping answers and the ARP table; the map's model and the channel lanes | Frame | Stays: the table parses in tens of milliseconds once per open, the rest is bounded by a /24 and 300 kept devices | `parse.test.ts`, `devices.test.ts` (a full sweep), `view.test.ts` |
| `notes` | Word and character count (`Intl.Segmenter`) over the whole note on open and on every key | Frame: 4 MiB about 200 ms a key | `ui/count-worker.js` after a 200 ms pause in typing; "Counting…" after 300 ms; a newer text cancels the count. The text area stays the engine's; at 4 MiB a key still costs about 130 ms more than a bare text area, Notes keeping its text, not counting (follow-up) | 4 MiB note (e2e: open 560–814 ms now, 277–390 ms after; a key 450–665 ms now, 229–275 ms after; on the CEF desk's Chromium alone an open 325–346 ms against the twin's fill of 215–229 ms, the engine laying out and drawing 4 MiB of text, which a text area already laid out costs as much, so the open's budget is the fill + 150 ms, #75, #100) |
| `notify` | None: a policy, no frame | — | — | — |
| `onboarding` | Scripted lines, one second of noise samples | Frame | None | — |
| `pack-github` | None: a pack, data only | — | — | — |
| `palette` | About 300 commands scored per key | Frame | Stays: 3–8 ms; a caller's pick list is uncapped (DOM, follow-up) | Read, not run |
| `permissions` | Approval previews, capped by the core (1 MiB, 200 lines) | Frame | Stays: bounded | — |
| `player` | FFmpeg (WebAssembly) software decoding | Worker (`ui/software/worker.js`) | Unchanged (confirmed); the lint keeps it there | PR #235 fixtures |
| `profiles` | None: a policy, no frame | — | — | — |
| `projects` | ≤ 2000 tasks; no parsing | Frame | Stays: the board's full redraw per key is DOM (follow-up) | 2000 tasks (Node) |
| `reader` | PDF (pdf.js), DjVu, comics | pdf.js worker; DjVu and comics in the library worker, the frame as fallback yielding every 25 ms | Unchanged (confirmed) | 300 MiB PDF (PR #240) |
| `screens` | VNC: noVNC's ZRLE, Tight, zlib and Hextile decoders, JPEG rectangles as base64 in JS | Frame (vendored noVNC: its RFB and Display need the DOM); H.264 decodes in WebCodecs | Stays for now; follow-up: the decoders in a worker drawing to an OffscreenCanvas | Read, not run |
| `session` | None: a policy, no frame | — | — | — |
| `sheets` | XLSX, ODS, CSV parsed | Worker (`ui/worker.js`), always | Unchanged (confirmed); the lint keeps `book.js`, SheetJS and the ODS reader there | 45.5 MB XLSX (PR #224) |
| `shell` | None: a policy, no frame | — | — | — |
| `sidebar` | One row per plugin | Frame | None | — |
| `slides` | PPTX, PPT, ODP parsed | Worker over 8 MiB, frame below | Unchanged (confirmed; out of scope) | PR #253 fixtures |
| `terminal` | PTY output (≤ 8 KiB an event) | Frame (xterm.js parses in 12 ms slices) | Stays: xterm yields; reflow and highlight-all search at 100k lines of scrollback are xterm's (follow-up) | Read, not run |
| `theme` | None: a policy, no frame | — | — | — |
| `theme-amber` | None: a theme, data only | — | — | — |
| `theme-fjord` | None: a theme, data only | — | — | — |
| `theme-glass` | None: a theme, data only | — | — | — |
| `theme-paper` | None: a theme, data only | — | — | — |

| `toolbar` | Drawers from the plugin list | Frame | None | — |
| `universal` | `desk.universal.open { file }` parses the whole file as YAML; data files through the SDK's loader (≤ 8 MiB) | Frame (the parsers are the SDK's: `yaml.ts`, `universal-load.ts`) | Stays for now; follow-up: the SDK's loader in a worker; view files are small | Read, not run |
| `views` | ≤ 500 items a component; templates | Frame | Stays: bounded (formatters built per placeholder, follow-up) | 500 items (Node) |

## Which change runs what

| You change | Runs on the PR |
| --- | --- |
| `crates/**` | `ci`: fmt, clippy, cargo test on Linux; `red-on-base` (new `crates/*/tests`); `security` |
| `apps/desk/shell/**`, `crates/desk-web/**`, `crates/desk-sys/**` | the above plus `shell-build` on three OSes (`just check-shell`, `just build-shell`; not a required check yet) |
| `packages/**`, `apps/**/src`, `plugins/**` | `ci`: biome, tsc, vitest and `runtime-e2e` on Linux; `red-on-base` (new `*.test.ts`); `security` |
| `crates/desk-compositor/**` | the `crates/**` row: in `cargo test -p desk-compositor`, the snapshots under the platform's tolerance, the hit-testing properties and the compose bench as a smoke test, on a GPU or lavapipe and WARP once #61 installs them; by hand before it lands, `just perf-compositor` on the reference machine for a change to drawing, the scene or text |
| `specs/surface/**`, `crates/desk-surface/**` | the `specs/**` row: the surface vectors in Rust and TypeScript (`crates/desk-surface/tests/vectors.rs`, `packages/bridge/src/surface.contract.test.ts`), `codegen-check`; the CEF shell job for the web host's lifecycle |
| `apps/desk/shell/src/web_host/**`, `apps/desk/shell/src/compositor_window/**`, `apps/desk/shell/src/probe/**`, `apps/desk/shell/src/pump.rs`, `apps/desk/shell/src/pump/**` | vitest for the page scripts (`web_host/frame_asks.test.ts`); the CEF shell job: `just build-shell`, `just check-shell` (the probe's `osr_import`, `lifecycle`, `isolation` and `a11y` tests); by hand before it lands, `just perf-compositor` on the reference machine (memory per live surface, the start to the first composed web surface, input to paint, presses CEF's busy-wait catches, presses on a desk at rest, the desk at rest) |
| `crates/desk-compositor/**`, `crates/desk-wm/**`, `apps/desk/shell/src/compositor_window.rs`, `apps/desk/shell/src/compositor_window/**`, `apps/desk/shell/src/web_host/**`, and every other change to how windows are placed, drawn, resized or painted: `apps/desk/shell/src/{window,main_frame,pane,app_windows}.rs`, `apps/desk/shell/src/app_windows/**`, `packages/runtime/src/shell/windows.ts`, `packages/runtime/src/surface/**` | the rows above, and by hand before it lands, `just perf-motion` green on the reference machine (ADR 0205's rule, ADR 0208, ADR 0213): the motion gate in Layers, whose sway (the app window grown and shrunk while a window inside it moves) is the test for every window change. A change that fixes a known failure takes it out of `known` in `e2e/perf/motion.json`, or out of `sometimes` once several gate runs pass it; one that moves a value past a limit names the measurement in its ADR |
| `apps/desk/shell/src/native_host/**`, `crates/desk-core/src/native/**`, `crates/desk-surface/src/native/**`, `examples/plugin-native-wgpu/**` | the `crates/**` row (`native_plugin.rs`, `desk-surface`'s native tests, the example's `tests/desk.rs` on macOS) and the CEF shell job (`just check-shell`); `just test-app-e2e --spec e2e/specs/native-plugin.spec.ts` on macOS before it lands |
| `e2e/perf/**` | `ci` (vitest runs `e2e/perf/lib/*.test.ts`, the budgets' judges); the perf recipes themselves by hand |
| `scripts/**` | `ci` (biome, `node --test`; `scripts/ci-workflows.test.mjs` checks which workflow runs when) |
| `.github/workflows/**` | `ci` (actionlint) |
| `.github/workflows/release*.yml`, `.github/workflows/canary.yml`, `.github/release-shell`, `scripts/release-*.mjs`, `scripts/cef-**`, `e2e/smoke/*.sh`, `apps/desk/shell/packager/**` | `release-dry-run` (every target built and signed with a throwaway key, the R2 tree written and verified, nothing uploaded; not a required check) |
| `scripts/ci-apt.mjs`, `scripts/ci-apt.test.mjs` | `ci` (Node fixture tests for source preservation, bounded APT retries/timeouts and workflow ordering) and `e2e` (the existing packaged-app tests after real Ubuntu dependency installation) |
| `specs/**` | `ci` (spec lint, contract tests), `codegen-check`; run `just codegen` and commit the generated files |
| `plugins/**`, `examples/**` | `ci` (biome for plugin JS; `examples/brain-echo` is covered by the Rust conformance and lease tests), `plugin-validate`; `plugins/**` also runs `e2e` |
| `e2e/**`, `plugins/**`, `apps/desk/**`, `crates/desk-sys/**`, `crates/desk-boot/**`, `crates/desk-web/**`, `.github/workflows/e2e.yml`, `scripts/ci-apt{,.test}.mjs` | `e2e` (`app-e2e` on three OSes: `just build-shell`, `just test-app-e2e`, `just smoke`; not a required check), in addition to the applicable `ci` rows above |
| `docs/**`, `*.md` | `ci`, `scope-check`; `review` skips them |
| any PR | `scope-check`, `labeler`; the required `ci` check is the aggregate of every `ci` job above |
| in the merge queue | `ci` again on the PR combined with `main` and the PRs ahead of it, on Linux (no `red-on-base`); `scope-check` reports success (ADR 0015) |
| a pull request into a release line `release/vX.Y` (a cherry-pick of main commits) | everything above plus check and unit tests on macOS and Windows and `shell-build` on all three OSes, and `release-dry-run` when it changes the release files, all inside the required `ci`; the `release` ruleset asks for a review and an up-to-date branch (ADR 0092) |
| a push to `release/vX.Y` (its cut or a merged cherry-pick) | `release.yml`: check and unit tests on every OS, then the line's next candidate `X.Y.Z-rc.<run>` built, uploaded and listed on beta |
| a published GitHub Release or `release-macos` dispatch | Both macOS images run the package smoke. With the updater key a version tag also carries signed packages and `UPGRADE_PATH.json` (ADR 0291). Publication is covered by `scripts/ci-release-macos.test.mjs` and `scripts/github-upgrade-path.test.mjs`. `crates/desk-update/tests/github.rs` covers discovery, compatible hops and verified offline installation. `crates/desk-core/tests/update.rs` and `apps/desk/src/updates.test.ts` cover one-time restart consent. |
| by hand: `release-cut`, `release-final`, `rollout`, `rollback` | a line cut from the canary build three days old; a final of the candidate three days on beta, tagged `vX.Y.Z` and listed on stable for 10% of the desks; a wider or stopped rollout; a build taken off. Each waits for a person in the `stable` environment (`docs/releasing.md`) |
| nightly | `canary.yml`: check and unit tests on macOS and Windows when `main` moved, and the canary build once the secrets exist (ADR 0081, ADR 0092) |

## Rules

- A task adds its tests to its own crate's or area's row of the layers table: one row per crate for
  Integration (Rust), one per area for Runtime (web), so parallel PRs touch different lines.
- Tests live next to the source. Acceptance tests for a task go in `crates/<crate>/tests/` or `*.test.ts`
  so red-on-base can run them on the base commit; inline Rust tests are reported but not checked there.
- Animations run on an injected clock and randomness takes a seed. Snapshots are taken at fixed times.
- Timing tests choose their own quiet periods, wide enough for a loaded runner, instead of relying on
  defaults (`watch_coalesces_a_burst_into_one_batch` debounces for 1 s, not the default 100 ms).
- Test helpers that build temp paths include a process-wide counter next to the process id, never the
  clock alone: tests run in parallel threads, and the clock can hand two of them the same path (#106).
- Test hooks (`desk.test.*`) exist only behind the `test-hooks` feature, never in release builds.
- Playwright runs only through `just test-e2e` and `just perf-opening` (ADR 0018, ADR 0144). The
  runtime e2e runs headless Chromium natively on every OS, four workers a run at most, on Chrome for
  Testing's headless shell of the CEF desk's own build (`packages/runtime/e2e/chromium.ts`, pinned in
  `e2e/driver/chrome-for-testing.json` and fetched on the first run; Playwright's bundled build of the
  same branch killed a frame's renderer when a just-created field took the focus); on macOS the
  Playwright configs refuse a run the runner did not start, and an agent's `--headed`, `--ui` or
  `--debug`, which open windows on WindowServer. One e2e invocation at a time per task. Measured on
  this change (#48): a full run with four workers kept 17 Chromium processes and 2.6 GB RSS at most,
  owned no WindowServer window, and left WindowServer's RSS flat. Render trees with text-sized boxes
  follow the fonts installed (two such snapshots read 34 and 541 px on macOS, 39 and 554 on Linux):
  the committed ones are Linux's, which CI gates on.
- The app e2e (WebdriverIO, not Playwright) runs through `just test-app-e2e` and `just smoke` on the
  CEF desk on macOS, Linux and Windows (ADR 0144; `just build-shell` first). Its `*.spec.ts` files are not seen by
  red-on-base, so a new app spec is proven by its pass in the `e2e` workflow on the PR.
- No test asks the OS for calendar or reminder access. The one EventKit test
  (`crates/desk-sys/tests/calendar.rs`, `on_macos_the_os_source_lists_real_calendars_when_access_is_granted`)
  is `#[ignore]`d because it reads the person's calendars: people run it on a Mac with access granted,
  `cargo test -p desk-sys --all-features --test calendar -- --ignored`.
- No test reads or writes the person's clipboard. The one OS clipboard test
  (`crates/desk-sys/tests/clipboard.rs`, `the_os_clipboard_writes_reads_and_marks_text`) is `#[ignore]`d
  because it replaces the clipboard for a moment: people run it on a Mac and on Windows before approving a
  change to the OS clipboard, `cargo test -p desk-sys --all-features --test clipboard -- --ignored`.
- No test reads this computer's proxy settings in the default run. The OS readers' tests
  (`crates/desk-sys/tests/net_proxy.rs`, `on_macos_system_settings_match_scutil` and
  `on_windows_system_settings_match_the_registry`) are `#[ignore]`d: people run them on a Mac and on
  Windows before approving a change to those readers,
  `cargo test -p desk-sys --all-features --test net_proxy -- --ignored`.
- No test opens a microphone or asks the OS for one. The capture tests use `FakeInput`, the OS test
  (`the_os_input_answers_on_this_platform`) lists devices and reads the grant without prompting, and the
  one real capture (`crates/desk-sys/tests/audio.rs`,
  `on_this_computer_the_os_input_captures_the_default_microphone`) is `#[ignore]`d: people run it with a
  microphone and the OS permission, `cargo test -p desk-sys --all-features --test audio -- --ignored`.
  Linux builds need the ALSA headers (`libasound2-dev`), which `check (linux)` installs.
- No test synthesizes input, performs an AX action, prompts for a TCC permission or captures the screen in
  the default run: computer use is tested through `FakeComputer`. The real-OS checks
  (`crates/desk-computer/tests/macos.rs`) are `#[ignore]`d and read only; people run them on a Mac before
  approving a change to the macOS driver, `cargo test -p desk-computer --test macos -- --ignored`, once
  with Accessibility and Screen Recording granted to the terminal and once without.
- No test posts an OS notification or asks the OS for notification permission: the notifier tests use
  `FakeNotifier`, macOS's only checks the refusal outside an app bundle, and the freedesktop tests talk
  to a server in the test process.
- A test that still flakes after three retries gets the `quarantine` label and an issue. Quarantined
  tests do not block merges but keep running nightly.
